# Implementation tracking

This is the single authoritative record for capability coverage, remaining implementation, historical comparisons, and batch handoffs. README contains library usage and links here; do not maintain a second backlog there.

Reviewed on **2026-10-18** against Siemens iX core **5.2.1**, commit `8bf599bc68365d7752e12832e71b625883b93d7a`, targeting Dioxus **=0.8.0-alpha.2**, web and desktop WebView. No component is certified as fully equivalent. The detailed [parity ledger](#parity-ledger) retains the existing bounded evidence and limitations.

## Current delivery requirements — user revised 2026-10-18

The target is **what Siemens iX Brand should be if built for Dioxus**, aiming to retain all nondeprecated functionality through idiomatic components, props, events and composition. A good visual approximation replaces exact rendering/runtime equivalence. Core interactions are the first priority and verification baseline, not the final feature ceiling. Missing user-facing capabilities remain explicit work rather than being silently dropped. Web/desktop support, the pinned version, licensing and cache preservation remain required.

A capability is ready when its intended features work through representative examples, its states look recognizably iX and its desktop/narrow layouts fit. Use focused functional tests, bounded visual checks and relevant repository gates. Preserve useful existing behavior and tests while simplifying or replacing implementation machinery. A targeted rewrite of overbuilt subsystems is acceptable; discarding the whole library is not assumed.

**Not required:** millisecond/frame chronology, identical DOM wrappers or invisible CSS values, copying JavaScript/custom-element API syntax, reproducing source quirks, or exhaustive internal/member/platform audits. These exemptions do not waive user-facing functionality. Missing advanced features remain candidates for Dioxus-native implementation; compatibility with foreign engine APIs need not dictate the architecture.

Historical parity comparisons remain reference material, not automatic blockers or audit tasks. Earlier statements treating advanced functionality as optional must be read under this clarification: preserve the functional breadth, adapt its implementation, and track genuine gaps. Do not restart microscopic comparisons.

## Immediate priority — user directed 2026-10-18

**R02 comes before every other task.** Use Dioxus's existing APIs first and simplify the handwritten JavaScript layer. A concrete missing or broken capability at the pinned version is required to justify retaining custom JavaScript; generic DOM/web/desktop explanations are insufficient. This applies to every agent, current task and future delegation. Preserve useful core workflows; exact source chronology and retained-object quirks are not requirements.

**Global rule: no JavaScript fallback paths**, for every component, agent and task. Custom content uses Dioxus composition or explicit mounted-element registration. Missing registration or legacy arbitrary-DOM compatibility does not justify JavaScript execution. Record unsupported capabilities instead of silently routing to JavaScript. Existing subsystems still awaiting R02 migration must follow this rule; do not interpret native integration exceptions as permission for a parallel fallback runtime.

**UI01 gallery work is deferred locally.** Its uncommitted navigation/search implementation and test evidence are preserved, but its remaining review, completion and delivery must not take precedence over R02. No new component, polish or broad audit batch should begin first.

## Updating this record

Use **Open**, **In progress**, **Blocked**, **Done**, and **Deferred** in the queue. An in-progress task names its batch owner in the handoff; a blocked task records its dependency and next action. Done requires evidence appropriate to the revised visible/core-interaction acceptance target.

1. Claim one bounded batch and record its owner, scope, source contract, acceptance criteria, and affected files before editing. One chat owns the active implementation batch.
2. Preserve IDs when splitting work; use suffixes such as G05.1 and G05.2. Add concrete tasks only when supported by source or observed behavior.
3. Update affected ledger rows with implementation status, exact verification/evidence references, separate web/desktop results, and remaining limitations. Keep historical evidence identified by its original batch.
4. Mark a queue item complete only when its criteria are met. Incomplete core work stays open with a concrete user-facing gap. Historical internal-equivalence gaps may be deferred; builds alone do not demonstrate working UI.
5. At handoff, record changed files, validation, active processes, unresolved work, and the exact next action under [Handoff](#handoff). Preserve supporting artifacts outside disposable build output when transferring the checkout.

## Parity ledger

This historical source-comparison record began at the v15 batch close on **2026-10-17**. Current acceptance is the approximation target above. No component is certified as fully equivalent, and that certification is no longer required. **Initial** means a counterpart exists; **partial** means named behavior is implemented with bounded evidence; **unimplemented** means no counterpart; **excluded** means outside the agreed scope. A fixture, API name, compilation, or passing unit test does not establish parity. “Not established” means no component-wide claim is supported here, even when a fixture renders.

### Inventory, separate from parity

- Pinned source: core **5.2.1**, commit `8bf599bc68365d7752e12832e71b625883b93d7a`; 123 declared custom-element tags, two deprecated tags excluded, **121 current tags / 120 initial Rust mappings**. `ix-playground` remains unimplemented.
- **256 current example adaptations** from 264 upstream preview filenames; eight legacy examples excluded. Availability is a fixture count.
- The read-only Rust audit inventories **830 properties, 36 methods, 122 events, 148 slots, and 3 CSS parts: 1,139 known current members** across 112 current tags with generated metadata. All generated member review statuses remain **unreviewed**. The named behavior evidence below is narrower than a completed member audit.
- Upstream generated metadata omits nine current internal tags: `ix-application-switch-modal`, `ix-css-grid`, `ix-css-grid-item`, `ix-date-time-card`, `ix-field-wrapper`, `ix-menu-expand-icon`, `ix-modal-loading`, `ix-tab-panel`, `ix-tab-set`. G05.1 recovered 40 additional source members below; Rust member review remains open. The audit checks the pinned revision and relevant upstream input cleanliness.
- Two deprecated active-tab properties on MenuAbout/MenuSettings and deprecated component members are excluded from current member counts. `SplitButtonItem`, `SelectOption`, and `TabItem` are supporting data types for current APIs and are allowed.

Member counts are inventory only, including slots/parts; zero with available metadata can be valid. “Metadata missing” is a contract-inventory gap, not proof of missing Rust implementation. Recovered inventory: **28 properties, 2 internal methods and 10 slots (40 members)** across nine tags; no declared events/parts. Combined known inventory is **1,179 members**, of which the original 1,139 still come from generated metadata. The audit utility continues reporting nine metadata omissions rather than fabricating generated completeness. Rust adaptations and differences require G05.2 review.

### Behavior evidence and remaining gaps

**Evidence keys:** W15 is the final frozen web build and browser-automation pass at 1440 × 1000 and 375 × 812; captures are in `target/review/*v15.png`. N15 is the successful full Windows WebView2 `native_bridge_probe` run logged in `target/review/v15-native.log`. W14/prior denotes earlier bounded browser checks retained in [README API notes](README.md#scope-and-parity) and ignored `target/review/` captures; those workflows were not all repeated in W15. N15 reran all previously registered native cases plus the new Menu/Tabs cases. Tests are implementation evidence, not rendered-platform evidence. Local logs/captures are supporting artifacts; this ledger is the durable record shipped with the crate.

Native checks use **synthetic DOM click/key/input events in a hidden WebView**, with actual `document.activeElement` assertions. A helper can deliver missing focus-family notifications; N15 needed **zero** such deliveries. Manual native tab activation explicitly clicks after arrow focus, so that case does not test trusted Enter/Space default activation. W15 separately exercised browser ArrowRight then Enter. Neither establishes physical operating-system keyboard input. Rapid asynchronous tab focus supersession/unmount remains unverified.

## Handoff

### R02.17 Menu/Category Dioxus keyboard ownership — delivered 2026-10-19

**Delivered:** typed Menu items and Category parents/children handle their keyboard actions directly through Dioxus even after native order observations arrive. A focused bare menubar owns container navigation; origin-less keys from arbitrary fields cannot trigger it. Accepted navigation prevents its default and stays in its owning scope. Root Up/Down/Home/End use current eligible observed order, including keyed reorders. Category entry, arrows, expanded Tab, Enter/Space close/parent return, Escape and pointer activation work without competing outer focus. Native editing/defaults remain; child Enter/Space does not activate the child action. Existing mounted focus, cues and lifetime guards are reused, with no JavaScript fallback.

Removed native Menu/Category key and click listeners, root focus reporting, key-path discovery, original-event transport/cancellation, key/default/return mirrors and ACKs, root-claim transactions and their exported attributes. The Rust model no longer has `MenuOrigin`, fallback-origin/native-key fields, `CategoryMirror`, `CategoryReturnAck` or `CategoryRootClaim`. Direct Category handling replaces duplicate preflight/default transactions; normal collapsed parent clicks continue presenting while expanded clicks toggle, as already implemented. Anchor tabindex writers are removed; canonical Dioxus links use MenuItem's href/attributes rather than a native wrapper repair.

**Remaining R02 boundary:** read-only ready/order/category-order facts still report current wrapped/keyed membership and eligibility. Pinned dioxus-html0.8.0-alpha.2 `MountedData` can focus/measure a registered element but does not enumerate descendant order; the bridge documents this gap. Category top-layer show/hide, hover, focusout/outside/removal facts and shared document discovery/geometry remain legacy work, not blanket claims of unavoidable JavaScript. Only outside/disconnect reports retain raw active-category ownership facts. Generic Dropdown/overlay native keyboard routing is separate work. Custom caller-authored slot propagation can differ from typed-control routing; native editing is verified, while expanded arbitrary-slot Escape and broader foreign composition remain unverified. No arbitrary-DOM fallback is added.

**Verification:**95 application tests pass as part of the full580-library +8 integration +3 doctest suite =591 tests. Editing check-lib, format, lint, full web check, desktop all-target check, native-preview Clippy and cached web/native builds pass. Included bridge/test files receive explicit rustfmt checking. Legacy-shaped fixture interactions now publish only order facts and dispatch actual Dioxus key/click/focus events. Useful order/disabled/hidden/default/lifetime/hover/removal/cue assertions remain; obsolete key/default ACK and root-claim assertions are removed or replaced with actual handler/focus assertions. New coverage verifies native-observed keys in both layouts, one Category destination, pointer activation, Escape return and custom-field native defaults.

Integration corrected four migrated fixture assumptions: collapsed clicks present instead of toggle-close; actual clicks no longer return a transport cancellation ACK; focus notifications do not bubble; caller-slot editing tests check native defaults/no traversal rather than incidental propagation. The native lifecycle journal now advances only on lifecycle facts, so old click-sequence expectations were corrected while keeping wrong-owner/replay/drop/replacement guards. The mixed runtime cue fixture now finds the rendered Category parent/id instead of the removed native return-target attribute. These are fixture adaptations; no timing delay or native fallback was introduced. The successful full-suite log supersedes earlier diagnostic failures.

Chromium actual input verifies menubar Home, root arrows/disabled-hidden skip, keyed reorder/current Home destination, collapsed Enter/Space entry and child return, expanded pointer opening, child entry/wrap/Tab and Enter/Space return with Last action remaining None. A real input retains Home/typing/ArrowDown and physical focus. Linux WebView's synthetic Menu replay additionally passes End/Up, current/restored keyed order, native field default prevention checks, Category both-mode navigation/Escape, hover return, child removal/remount and owner remount with zero cue writes. Application focus, cues, opening, triggers, exits, mixed, generic-focus and Modal regressions pass. Synthetic events do not establish physical OS input or AT behavior.

One bounded visual pass checks a1000px iframe viewport scaled for display and a360px unscaled viewport. Navigation scrolls, the editing field and independent workspace remain reachable, content fits, and no visual repair was needed. Large application tests move to `src/application/tests.rs`; native bridge constants/function move to included `src/application/menu_dom.rs` for exclusive parallel ownership. Public APIs/module paths remain unchanged. The lead's runtime file edit changes only the two invalidated Publication test lookups. The staged-source package audit verifies bytes/frozen hashes and excludes development tools/deferred gallery work. Contract, focused/full gates, native/browser facts, screenshots, hashes and package evidence are in `target/review/r02-menu-keyboard/`; port8119 serves `?menufocus`. Unrelated AGENTS/Cargo/hook/gallery changes remain preserved.

**Next:** continue R02 replacing Category hover/outside/shared discovery and top-layer/geometry machinery with explicit Dioxus relationships, events and mounted APIs; retain only demonstrated native gaps. Then address remaining overlay key/inventory and keyed-move repair. Keep the Pane facts/metrics/outside and multi-Pane limitations recorded in R02.16. The global no-JavaScript-fallback rule applies to all tasks/agents. R02 and the overall counterpart remain incomplete; gallery/new components/polish stay deferred.

### R02.16 Settings/About and Pane mounted focus — delivered 2026-10-19

**Delivered:** Menu Settings/About register their utility invokers and Close controls. Explicit Rust selection/lifetime state and Dioxus focusin/out replace `menu_focus_bridge`, its document observer, global focus listener and native restoration. Opening focuses Close; Close/Escape return to the captured live utility. Direct content switching keeps the original invoker; external close after outside focus preserves that independent destination. Vetoes, newer visibility, replacement and owner teardown reject obsolete work. Menu overlay focus needs no native query and has no JavaScript fallback.

Pane autofocus, Tab/ShiftTab boundary wrapping and eligible owned opener return use existing weak registered Dioxus mounted snapshots. The built-in toggle and library Button/IconButton controls register automatically. Custom fields/actions use `use_focus_mount(id)` in a component that unmounts with the control. Unregistered controls retain ordinary native use but receive no programmatic focus or JavaScript fallback. Interior Tab/editing remain native; inline panes do not trap. Outside dismissal preserves an independent destination. Owner/epoch/presentation/mount/document guards reject stale work on replacement, closure, mode changes and teardown.

Pane's native focus/traversal executors, global DOM focus stack, frame scheduler, mutation/disconnection observers and document focus-repair listener are removed. A bounded read-only query reports eligible existing IDs, current active ID, visibility/inertness, autofocus and nested-overlay facts. Installed dioxus-html0.8.0-alpha.2 `events/mounted.rs` supplies focus/measurement but no active-control or descendant enumeration; `events/focus.rs` serializes no target/relatedTarget. These are the documented capability gaps, not a fallback executor. Dioxus lifecycle/focus events refresh the cache; `data-pane-focus-current` exposes committed current-ID readiness for synthetic events without a timing tracker. Legacy parent metrics/resize and outside-click delivery remain separate R02 work, not claims of unavoidable JavaScript.

**Limits:** forced outside-focus repair and global multi-Pane stacking are not adapted. Ordinary single-Pane Tab boundaries and nested overlay keyboard ownership remain; useful multi-Pane coordination needs a future Dioxus implementation. Rapid synthetic focus+Tab waits for committed facts; actual Chromium Tab passes independently. Already-issued renderer focus cannot be recalled. Full native physical input, AT, other platforms and complete application APIs remain unverified.

**Verification:**94 focused application tests pass. Four new actual Menu tests cover autofocus/Close/Escape, direct switch/outside preservation, veto/replaced invokers and queued work/owner teardown. Three mounted Pane tests cover autofocus/boundary/native interior behavior, unsupported destinations/nested/inline ownership, return eligibility/outside preservation and replacement/close/teardown. The obsolete pure Pane index helper/test is removed, with wrapping covered by the actual controller. All579 library +8 integration +3 doctests =590 tests pass, plus editing check-lib, formatting, lint, full web check, desktop all-target check, native-preview Clippy and cached web/native builds. Included Pane/tests receive explicit rustfmt checking in addition to Cargo format.

Test integration corrected an event type and SSR numeric ampersand matching; the excluded preview's format string was repaired before successful gates. No timer, native fallback or replacement scheduler was added.

Linux WebView's synthetic application replay passes Settings/About opening/Close/Escape/switch/outside/veto/editing and Pane autofocus/editing/boundaries/interior defaults/Close/Escape/outside/inline/remount. Existing Menu, cues, opening, trigger, exit, mixed, generic-focus and Modal replays pass. Native synthetic events do not establish physical Tab traversal; Chromium supplies that evidence.

One bounded visual pass checks Settings and Pane at1000px (iframe scaled for display) and360px unscaled. Content fits and focused actions remain reachable; no visual repair was needed. Staged-source packaging verifies archive bytes/frozen hashes and excludes development tools/deferred gallery work. Contract, pinned API excerpts, gates, native/browser facts, screenshots, hashes and package audit are in `target/review/r02-application-focus/`. Port8119 serves `?applicationfocus`; unrelated hook/AGENTS/Cargo/gallery changes remain preserved.

### R02.12 Dioxus-owned Modal return focus — delivered 2026-10-19

**Delivered:** Modal typed click/keyboard openers register their actual Dioxus controls and capture the trigger actually clicked, including multiple triggers for one controller. The obsolete `remember_focus` argument is removed from shared native activation and Popover opening.

Pinned Dioxus MountedData supplies `set_focus` but no active-element query or native `showModal`/`showPopover` operation. The remaining native opener observation reads only `document.activeElement`'s existing ID, then Rust resolves a live registered snapshot before native presentation. Native top-layer activation remains narrowly documented beside that operation; it never invents a return target or executes focus. Browser dialog initial autofocus and containment remain native behavior. Full library JS removal is not claimed.

### R02.3 Dioxus-owned Input measurement and Select popup layout — 2026-10-18

**Delivered:** Input removes its measurement observer stream entirely. Framework `onresize` supplies initial and changed border-box widths directly; Rust renders the corresponding padding. There are no DOM lookups, async measurement queries, retained-handle registry, resize/mutation/removal observers or RAF loop in input_geometry.rs. Prefix/suffix changes, password reveal, disabled controls and controlled typing remain usable.

Select replaces `SELECT_POPOVER_BRIDGE` with Rust placement and declarative styles, mounted anchor/popup geometry, scroll-size queries and framework resize events. Dioxus owns surface lifetime and stale-result guards. CSS resolves authored width/max-width values; Rust positions against actual mounted width rather than parsing CSS lengths. Content size determines whether the larger space above is preferable to a clipped list below. This is a reasonable placement adaptation, not exact upstream full-fit/timing behavior. Current width/content/anchor changes remeasure without remounting the open surface. No useful feature was intentionally removed.

**Specific native exceptions:** pinned MountedData/RenderedElementBacking exposes element focus/scroll/rect methods, but no `showPopover({source})`, viewport/visualViewport facts, computed inherited direction, or document/ancestor scroll/style-change subscription. Select therefore retains three small bindings:6 physical lines for native activation,10 for viewport/direction facts and11 for invalidation subscriptions (27 total, down from58). Invalidation observes current ancestor dir/style/class and viewport/document scrolling; it neither computes geometry nor writes styles. Dioxus cleanup ends the subscription. Native activation errors retain the inline fallback and callback; unsupported browser engines were not separately certified. Existing small text-selection/focus/form bindings remain for their recorded gaps.

WorkflowSteps retains its narrow DOM-order observer: pinned core's component traversal needs unavailable internals/VirtualDom access, keyed moves can skip rendering and onmounted, and MountedData has no tree/document-order API. Registration or render order would break wrapped/nested/keyed composition. The bridge now documents this source-backed gap; no Workflow behavior change is claimed.

### G04.5 root document modality lifetime — named contract delivered 2026-10-18

Owner this chat (`01a1497a-f870-7ef2-a8ba-5daa7a81e114`); full cloning stays active. Previous turn pushed completed work/progress as `8c5420b` to `main`. **Source contract:** pinned `utils/internal/mixins/setup.mixin.ts24–29,144–169` initializes one module detector at first componentDidLoad and never destroys it on disconnect. `utils/focus/detect-keyboard-mode.ts16–56` listens at document bubble; `focus-utilities.ts17–30` defines all12 exact keys. Recognized keys set true; mousedown/touchstart reset; other keys/pointer movement preserve mode. stopPropagation blocks document reach; preventDefault alone does not veto detection. Every recognized event emits source keyboardmodechange, including repeated unchanged values. Category dismissal captures focused child ancestry and authorizes its two-frame return before hide as previously established. Ten cached source files match pinned upstream bytes (`source-integrity.json`); no upstream rebuild.

**Implementation:** `src/application.rs` owns root modality and a document sequence independent of Menu pointer sequences. One root task receives raw batches with weak coordinator references, retains mode across Menu removal and notifies live categories. `src/menu_hover_dom.rs` owns document bubble listeners and an unacknowledged raw input journal; it neither filters focus keys nor stores modality policy. Each event synchronously flushes all pending facts into connected owner-tagged Menu inputs before Window handlers, then sends the same batch to the root stream. Newly mounted inputs drain before their document-ready marker; the roving bridge also synchronously bootstraps before hover listener installation. Rust accepts facts in order, rejects wrong-root/stale/duplicate batches and ACKs processed sequences; native plumbing trims only acknowledged facts. This avoids losing an earlier gap Tab/reset when a newer irrelevant key reaches a live Menu first. The unacknowledged transport queue has no arbitrary truncation; it depends on receiver progress.

The receiver task outlives its initiating Menu/provider component and its Eval guard sends finish at root teardown. Native preflight caught a real alpha.2 integration error: Desktop Window/Portal provides Document below ROOT, so looking it up inside the root task selected NoOpDocument. The fix captures the renderer provider in the Menu effect, then evaluates through that captured provider in the root task. Evaluator storage remains renderer-owned; the root task/guard establishes our receiver/cleanup lifetime. Source module-global initialization differs from this lazy per-VDOM adapter, including multiple roots/windows and events before the initial listener is ready. Automatic recovery after a failed channel is unproven: a same-owner restart can reset native sequence against the retained Rust watermark and overlap asynchronous registry cleanup. These remain explicit gaps.

Helpers exclusively owned the two new excluded source/port drivers, native helper and dedicated fake-provider test at separate phases; reports stayed ignored. No styling/visual edits or Impeccable pass; prior exact-file filter typography false-positive ignore is unchanged, with no new suppression or standing design finding. Observed source contract (bounded Chromium fixture):

| Path | Observation and implication for B10.6 |
| --- | --- |
| Focus-only entry | Tab from before control focuses input with popup closed. Source input focus itself does not open it. |
| Closed input ArrowDown | Stops+prevents; opens then focuses first native .dropdown-item next frame. Category Vendor and suggestion Apple observed; top-layer Vendor also observed. |
| Open category ArrowDown | Focuses first matching category/value native button, stops propagation without preventing default. Alpha observed. No cyclic input active index. |
| Open suggestions ArrowDown | Plain buttons lack .category-item selectors. Falls through to form; wrapped host has no next sibling, so input stays focused and default is prevented. Distinct from closed path. |
| Legacy form arrows | ArrowUp reads document.activeElement.previousSibling; unmatched open ArrowDown uses nextSibling. Deep active input retargets to outer shadow host. Direct adjacent before/after buttons receive focus. Text-node or different consumer topology is not interchangeable. |
| Native option keys | Category/value/suggestion native Enter/Space is prevented by shared active-descendant listener, with no click/selection. Native buttons are excluded from ix-only current/arrow selectors; option arrows/Home/End do not move to another native option. This is an observed source quirk, not evidence for normal roving navigation. |
| Literal input Space | Closed path opens and focuses first option, inserting no space; already-open input remains empty and cancellation occurs in shared handler. Retain as observed source quirk. |
| Typed input Enter | Commits current draft, not a highlighted suggestion. Ban emitted Ban while Banana was available; no extra category callback. Input focus retained. |
| Input Home/End | Stop propagation only, preserving native caret/selection and focus. Closed Home moved to0; shifted End selected0–6; open End/ShiftHome kept selection0–6 and popup open. |
| Tab | Native input→reset→operator order when preview exists; input/reset are outside popup trap. First popup option ShiftTab wraps last; last Tab wraps first (prevented, propagation does not reach host/document bubble). Inline and top-layer observed. |
| Escape | Input form closes before shared host handling, bubbles without preventDefault and retains input focus. Option/operator Escape stops without preventing default, closes; hiding focused control plus nonfocusable trigger restoration leaves Chromium body focused. Do not implement an explicit body focus command. |
| Pointer selection/operator | Trusted Vendor selects, focuses input before categoryChanged; trusted Apple emits one filterChanged while callback still sees Apple focus, then input receives focus. Both keep popup open. Dropdown closeBehavior=outside prevents bubbling click before trigger toggle (earlier close speculation withdrawn). Operator pointer toggles and stays focused on operator, with no category/filter notification. Request scheduling and native focus delivery must be compared separately in Rust. |

### B10.4 CategoryFilter chronological tokens — closed 2026-10-17

Changed: `src/patterns.rs`, new `examples/gallery/filter_order_review.rs`, gallery integration, excluded development-only `tools/filter_order_probe.rs`/Cargo target, and this ledger. Stable occurrence-bearing entries retain interactive and accepted programmatic chronology while public FilterState remains partitioned. Equal category duplicates have distinct DOM identity; pointer/Enter/Space close removes only its occurrence, prevents default/propagation and requests no input focus. Other non-Tab chip keys are prevented/stopped without removing; disabled/readonly omit close controls. Empty-input Backspace clears preview silently retaining operator, otherwise removes the actual last occurrence. Successful commits append, reset draft silently and emit one onchange without category(None); typed Enter leaves popup state untouched. Interactive trim/duplicate/operator/unique behavior follows pinned source rather than public helper policies. Source staticOperator controls display/button availability, while accepted token uses retained internal operator; uniqueCategories only filters choices.

Controlled adapters retain authoritative entries and the latest pending proposal only. Matching parent projection acknowledges its known order; rejected changes retain prior displayed occurrences. Unrelated split state reconstructs plain-then-category order. Older acknowledgements without retained proposal history also reconstruct canonically; equal projections cannot encode acknowledgement intent or different interleaving, so the latest matching proposal wins. Direct default/controlled categories preserve declared kind (including reserved IDs); source-like commands classify reserved custom IDs as plain. Public grouped FilterState helpers remain adapters. All order/draft/focus/scroll revisions are stored before FIFO notifications; nested commands retain final state. Unconsumed append scrolling retargets a removal, but standalone removal creates no new request.
